# CLAIMS LEDGER (paper/CLAIMS_LEDGER.md)

Maps every quantitative claim of `paper/sections/*.md` to the file that supports it. Created 2026-10-23 (P2) from three independent read-only audits of the sections (parts A, B, C below) plus a reference check (part D). Paths are relative to the repository root. Numbers in the generated tables (`paper/tables/*.md`) come from `paper/scripts/make_tables.py` and the data files it reads (`reports/data/*.csv|json`, `paper/data/*`); the final run is read from `paper/data/final2_metrics.jsonl` (re-copied read-only from the GPU box on 2026-10-23 after the run ended, 439 lines, last step 401; an earlier copy had 298 lines and ended at step 267; steps 251-267 appear up to three times because of two restarts and the paper uses the last, resumed lineage; Part E adds the 2026-10-23 additions of P3).

**Status vocabulary.** VERIFIED: value in the paper matches the cited source at the shown precision. DERIVED: computed from cited sources (formula given). UNSOURCED: no file in the repository supports it (an owner statement, external fact, or negative statement). MISMATCH: the source differs; all MISMATCH rows of the audits were corrected in the text on 2026-10-23 (see "Resolution" below), the rows are kept as found. **Section numbers in the audit rows follow the numbering at audit time**, in which parts of the text were numbered one lower (ethics 11, reproducibility 12, limitations 10, discussion 9, conclusion 13); the current numbering is: 1 Introduction, 2 Related work, 3 Data and SFT, 4 Method, 5 Setup, 6 Results, 7 Reward analysis, 8 Research question, 9 Case studies, 10 Discussion, 11 Limitations, 12 Ethics, 13 Reproducibility, 14 Conclusion.

## Resolution of the audit findings (2026-10-23)

Corrected in the text: zero-shot status and count of the 8 non-RL languages (3.2); fertility statement is a token ratio over 54 languages (3.3); bin shares 20-29% (3.4); shaping slope sentence (4.3); length-ratio bound (4.3); draw-weight range (4.1); `max_model_len` / `max_new_tokens` by phase (App. B); calibration "21 of 41" attributed to the first calibration (App. C); NLLB-coverage count (App. A caption); stable-range sentence of the dev macro (6.3); IbomMT numbers attributed to the right models (6.6); dev-set description (25 papers x 6 languages, 542-1,346 tokens; 13 weak-dev languages) (6.7, 10); minimum detectable change instead of "noise 0.4" (7.7, 8.2, 10, 11); Gemma-4-31B "not better for 21, level for 4" (7.5, 9.2); NLLB margins list (7.5); grad-norm sentence (7.4); Ge'ez statements (8.1, 9.1: "among the weakest", finished rate 0.76-0.94, "at most +0.6" qualified, 24-36% data increase, C3 token addition, Ge'ez-specific comparisons separated from A2W); Latin-script tokenizer range (9.1); Urhobo failure-cause sentence (9.3); NLLB use for five weak-signal languages (9.2); 8-14B LLMs are ahead only on the clean subset (10); JW 58% (+Bible 62%); weak-group chrF++ range (11, 12); headings renumbered; Afrique baseline description (5); statement on TranslatePsy hedged (6.2); `kl_coef` 0.1 from `final2_config.json`.
Left as stated, flagged here: (i) the wind-down plan "about step 400-450", the rate 1.19 USD per hour (the earlier cost estimate used 1.034), the ablation budget of 20 USD and the tokenizer figures "2.07x vs 3.9-7.5x" are statements of the project lead without a file in the repository (TODO(owner) in the text where applicable); (ii) NFD "up to 1.29 times" tokens and the vetting rule "bottom 15% per language, QE below 0.20" rest on project memory notes, not on a report; (iii) "four attention heads, one key-value head" is from the public Gemma 3 270M configuration, not from a repository file; (iv) the negative literature claim (no earlier RL-trained ~270M African MT model with 2,048-token prompts) rests on about 28 searches on 2026-10-22/23 (`lit/notes_*.md` for the earlier ones); (v) "never trained" for the data-scaling probe and "every RL configuration was run once" are absence-of-evidence statements; (vi) the `val_macro` column (1.3025 to 1.2654) of `paper/data/sft_eval.csv` is the 77-language macro validation loss quoted in the paper (the `val_loss` column is a different quantity).

Abbreviations: F2D = `paper/data/final2_eval/` (copy in `reports/data/final2_eval_2026-10-23/`); MCW = `reports/data/model_comparison_computed/*_with_final2.csv` (copy in `paper/data/model_comparison_computed/`); box paths are read-only inputs (`/workspace/jobs/e2/out_final2`, `out_final2_other`, `scores/final2*.pkl`, `/workspace/jobs/a4/eval_final2_step401`, `eval_final1433`, `/workspace/jobs/r7/eval/{A2W/alpha_0.70, A2W_a0.7_test, final_1433, final_1433_test}`, `/workspace/jobs/e1/sys_{sft,rl}.jsonl.gz`). All statistics were computed on the CPU of the box (no GPU job started; `ab2`, `r7`, `datagen` untouched); scripts `paper/scripts/box_e2_model_comparison_aggregate_with_final2.py` (E2 aggregation with several reference models), `box_p5_bench_compare.py`, `box_p5_afs_compare.py`. Intervals: 95% percentile, paired bootstrap, B = 1,000, sentences resampled (papers at paper level), no language-sampling noise, one run per system. ## Part M: removed from the paper by owner decision 2026-10-25 (P10)
The TRIAGE-score analysis (the difficulty classifier, score bands, the benchmark study `reports/triage_difficulty_vs_quality_2026-10-23.md`, the real-data 200-sample check `reports/real_data_band_check_200_samples_2026-10-24.md`, and the "dataset to translate" filtering advice) is not part of this research. It was never entered in the ledger rows above (the short paper's "Real-data check, and the triage score" paragraph of the Analysis section was listed only under REVIEW_CHECKLIST.md section 3 as "content not in the ledger"). Removed from the short paper and the checklist on 2026-10-25; the numbers it carried (AUC 0.42 [0.34, 0.51]; failure 3.3-8.9% per band; QE AUC 0.70-0.73; filter 6.4%; Amharic self-consistency 27 vs 53-74; about -0.02 AfriCOMET per score point above 3.5) are no longer claims of the paper. The reports stay in `reports/` as research records. Rows C122 and C131 mention a "band" (SD band of group rewards; QE-unit band between anchors) and are unrelated.

**Deviations from H1 and precision notes (flagged, CSV value used).** (1) H1 section 1 gives boilerplate "consensus-only 2.29% (RL-final) to 2.45% (final2)"; `boilerplate_by_system.csv` has 13 languages for RL-final and 11 for the final checkpoint, so that pair is unpaired; the paired file gives -0.25 pp [-0.41, -0.08]. The paper uses the paired value. (2) H1 section 5 says wrapper-mode unfinished "about 1%": 0.6-1.2% over all documents, 1.3-3.5% at about 2,048 tokens. (3) H1 section 3 quotes the copy-penalised slope of -1.2 pp per 100 steps for steps 151-401 next to first/last values of the whole run; the paper states both windows. (4) H1 lists wrong-language as not marked; the CSV verdict is "worse" (+0.05 pp [+0.01, +0.10]); the paper states it. (5) The `tables/` directory on disk dated from 2026-10-23 and lacked the final-checkpoint rows of the long-context tables and the judge-pilot tables; `make_tables.py` regenerated them from the data in the repository (the final2 rows now present agree with the text of Section 6.10). (6) Section numbers: the new subsection is 7.9; 7.1a (judge pilot) is not touched.

**Changed statements (before -> after).** (1) 7.1a "Planned, not yet done ... Until then the paper contains no judge score for any of our models" -> the judge was applied (USD 5.42), results in 6.12. (2) 7.1a "the reference-free prompt ... is not validated" -> validated in Run 1 (pooled 0.494 [0.394, 0.586]; usable for six of eight languages). (3) 7.1a "we plan to use 3.8 Flash" -> "we used". (4) 6.10 caveat (ii) "no human or LLM judge of discourse quality was used" -> no human judge of discourse; the LLM judge scores aligned units of one to a few sentences, not discourse. (5) 6.10 claim table, row "our model translates documents up to about 2,048 tokens": "partly: stable, but omits 12-30%" + the judge score. (6) 6.10 "Wording we use": new closing sentences with the judge result (no existing sentence removed). (7) 7.9 limitation (4) "no human or LLM judgement of these outputs is used" -> LLM judge used only for whole-document units. (8) Limitations 6: "has not been applied to our models' output, so it adds no evidence" -> applied; at Spearman about 0.5; not a substitute; item 7: "no human or LLM judge of discourse" -> as (4); new item 15. (9) Abstract: "human references and metrics that correlate with human judgement (AfriCOMET, SSA-COMET)" -> adds "and an independent LLM judge (Spearman about 0.5 with human ratings)"; the statement "there is no human evaluation" is kept. (10) Short paper: the pilot paragraph (Pro/Flash comparison, "no judge score of our models is reported here") replaced by the results paragraph; Limitations 1 "has not yet been applied to our models" removed; abstract clause as (9).

**Discrepancies between the J2 report and the CSV files (CSV used).** (1) Run 2 per-language intervals: the report's table takes them from `run2_judge_by_language.csv` (e.g. amh [3.19, 6.87]); `run2_same_sentences_metric_differences.csv` row `judge` has marginally different bootstrap limits (amh [3.195, 6.820]; macro [3.223, 4.860] against [3.215, 4.857]), i.e. a second resampling; the paper uses the first file. (2) Alignment Spearman of the judge score with the own-block score: the CSV has 0.7446 (final checkpoint, whole document) and 0.5746 (RL-final), which round to 0.74 and 0.57; the J2 report prints 0.75 and 0.58 (rounding error in the report); the paper uses 0.74 and 0.57. (3) The J2 section 5 paragraph uses six-language macros (24 / 65 / 85 / 17%); the paper uses the five common languages (25.8 / 67.0 / 85.6 / 16.4%) because TranslatePsy has no Kinyarwanda output, and gives the six-language macros (23.9 / 65.4 / 85.3 / 17.3%) in the table. (4) The J2 report gives the RL-final block size as 1.2; the CSV mean is 1.25. (5) The judge report says "max thinking 912" for all runs; Run 1 max is 727 and Run 2 875 (the paper says 912 as the maximum over all runs). (6) Run 1 mean scores and the 0.954 correlation are computed on 300 segments including Somali and Xhosa (unusable pairs); no change. No other disagreement between the report and the CSVs was found.

(5) Ledger Part N says the pilot's written report was not saved in `reports/`; it is now (`reports/judge_pilot_2026-10-25.md`); its numbers agree with the CSVs (S2-S4).

The paper's old abstract number 3.7 was the paired deficit over the 43 NLLB-covered languages (RL-final 34.7 against 38.4, `t_cmp_main`, Section 6.8 "The 43-language set"); it still stands there (3.7 [3.6, 3.8]) and is no longer in the abstract, Section 1 or Section 14. The old intro sentence "3.9 chrF++ behind NLLB-200 3.3B on published numbers" (Section 6.2, all rows) was replaced by the identical-sentence headline; Section 6.2 itself is unchanged. (2) The owner's brief calls amh and tir "largest single gaps": per language, Kabuverdianu (kea) is the largest gap to NLLB-3.3B (20.98 against 19.00 and 10.97) and Tigrinya is third; against the 600M model Amharic is largest (18.31) and kea second. The papers therefore say "outlier (with Kabuverdianu, the largest single gaps)" and state that Kabuverdianu is kept in the headline set. (3) The brief gives amh / tir values to one decimal (19.0 vs 38.0 vs 37.3; 13.8 vs 24.7 vs 22.4): they agree with the CSV (18.95, 37.96, 37.26; 13.76, 24.73, 22.37). (4) Section 9.1 said 262,145 vocabulary entries (the model's embedding rows, Section 3.3 and 6.11 keep 262,145 for the output head); it now says 262,144 tokenizer entries for the Ethiopic count, as in the vocabulary-pruning report. (5) Short Appendix D previously said "script, tokenisation, data and capacity are all candidates"; replaced by the four candidates with the pretraining coverage added.

(2) The language name for `run` is Kirundi (Rundi); the papers write Kirundi. (3) "Gain" over SFT-final for the 45th language (Makhuwa, no NLLB output) is not in the 44-language mean; the abstract's +5.4 is the 45-language macro (+5.42). (4) No new GPU work and no new data; no triage and no TranslatePsy-derived training samples are mentioned.


## Part V: Contamination audit applied (P17, 2026-10-26)

Source of truth: `reports/benchmark_contamination_audit_2026-10-26.md` (agent C1; executive summary and section 4 with 17 replacement wordings) and `reports/data/contamination_audit_2026-10-26/` (CSV and JSON; where the audit text and a file differ, the file was used). Evidence labels as in the audit: VERIFIED (read from a scan output, configuration, script or the Hub), REPORTED, UNKNOWN (kept UNKNOWN in the papers). Long paper: abstract, Section 1 (item 2, new item 9, outline), 3.4, 3.6, 4.3, 6.1, 6.2 (and tables `t_published`, `t_published21`), 6.3, 6.7 (and table `t_final2_bench`), 6.8, 6.9, **new Section 6.9a** (tables `t_exposure_stages`, `t_exposure_bench`, `t_exposure_size`, `t_exposure_nllb`, `t_exposure_highlights`, generated by `paper/scripts/make_exposure.py`), 6.12 (caveat 7), 7.3, 10 (H1, H4, item on "larger on the clean subset"), 11 (items 1, 4, 10, 15(d), new 17), 13, 14, Appendix C (Efik calibration data). Short paper: abstract, Intro (contribution 2, scope), Setup (evaluation sets paragraph rewritten), Results 5.1 (Table 1 restructured: `clean` block first, `no_pair` block second; group block removed, numbers inline), 5.2, 5.4, Limitations (item 7 rewritten, new item 12), Conclusion, Appendix A, **new Appendix H** (Tables `tab:exposure`, `tab:exposuresize`) and Appendix I (ablation table and figure and the long-input table, moved from the main text to keep it within 9 pages). Not added, by instruction: triage scores, any TranslatePsy-derived training samples.

**What was not applied or is doubted.** (1) The audit's size figure "about 3.2 and 2.0 after the 0.6 difficulty of clean sentences" uses the untrained base model's FLORES+ difference (13.57 against 12.96) from the step-20,500 SFT report; it is stated as REPORTED and as an approximation (the base model is not SFT-final). (2) "NLLB-200 used FLORES as its development set" is the audit's statement (and the common knowledge that FLORES-200 was made for NLLB); it is cited to the NLLB paper but was not re-read there (REPORTED). (3) The highlight table of Section 6.9a uses the generation-run pipeline (`bench_boot.csv`: Kabuverdianu +14.59 on all rows) while the abstract and Section 6.8 use the identical-sentence pipeline (+14.2); the difference is at most 0.53 chrF++ per language (ledger U10) and is said in the caption; the audit's own highlight table uses the first pipeline. (4) The audit's per-language scale estimates for the SFT sample have intervals that span an order of magnitude; the text says so and uses them only for "about 8,300 windows". (5) The 5-of-800 demonstration check (V21) assumes that the Run 2 `sid` is the FLORES+ devtest row index; the check cannot distinguish a real overlap from chance and is stated as such. (6) No result was recomputed without the test-fitted reward components (no run exists); the papers say so. (7) The audit lists "how the 17 weakest languages x2.5 were chosen" as UNKNOWN; kept UNKNOWN. (8) The audit's remark that the carriers of the Luganda, Hausa, Yoruba and most Igbo FLORES+ rows must be among the private older repositories is inference and is labelled so ("circumstantial"). (9) The audit's protocol (section 5) and its filter script are mentioned in one sentence of each paper as not applied to these models; they are not described as part of this work. (10) The statement "Efik half of IbomMT is affected" is bounded by the audit: the effect size is unknown; the Ibibio-against-Efik comparison is explicitly not a test.
